First, let me identify the protein. It&#x27;s described as a mannan endo-1,4-beta-mannosidase (also known as beta-mannanase or mannosidase). This is a glycoside hydrolase enzyme.

Glycoside hydrolases typically use one of two catalytic mechanisms:
1. **Inverting mechanism**: Single displacement - one glutamate acts as a general base (activates water), the other as a general acid (protonates the leaving group).
2. **Retaining mechanism**: Double displacement - one glutamate acts as a nucleophile (attacks the anomeric carbon), the other as a general acid/base (first protonates the leaving group, then activates water).

For beta-mannanases (GH family 26 and GH family 5), the mechanism is typically **retaining**, meaning one glutamate is the nucleophile and the other is the proton donor/acid.

Now, let me look at the sequence context around positions 204 and 318.

Let me count the positions in the sequence:

For GH26 enzymes specifically, looking at literature:
- The nucleophile is the first glutamate (upstream)
- The acid/base is the second glutamate (downstream)
